   47: 
   48: # Compiled once at module load; reused in every identifier-safety check.
   49: # Using \A/\z (true string anchors) rather than ^/$ which match around \n.
   50: # SAFE_IDENTIFIER: bare SQL identifier â€” letters, digits, underscore.
   51: # SAFE_QUALIFIED:  allows a single dot for table.column notation in JOINs.
   52: my $SAFE_IDENTIFIER = qr/\A[a-zA-Z_][a-zA-Z0-9_]*\z/;
   53: my $SAFE_QUALIFIED  = qr/\A[a-zA-Z_][a-zA-Z0-9_.]*\z/;
   54: 
   55: # Type-inference regexes and sample size for schema() when infer_types => 1.
   56: # Compiled once at load; reused in _infer_type() across all schema() calls.
   57: use constant INFER_TYPE_SAMPLE_SIZE => 100;
   58: my $INFER_INT_RE  = qr/\A-?\d+\z/;
   59: my $INFER_REAL_RE = qr/
   60: 	\A              # start of string
   61: 	-?              # optional leading minus
   62: 	\d+             # integer part -- required
   63: 	(?:\.\d+)?      # optional decimal part
   64: 	(?:             # optional exponent block:
   65: 		[eE]        #   e or E marker
   66: 		[+-]?       #   optional sign
   67: 		\d+         #   exponent digits
   68: 	)?
   69: 	\z              # end of string
   70: /x;
   71: # No \z anchor: intentionally matches any valid timestamp prefix so that full
   72: # timestamps with seconds, fractional seconds, or timezone offsets are still
   73: # classified as TIMESTAMP -- e.g. 2024-01-01 12:34:00.000+05:30
   74: my $INFER_TS_RE   = qr/
   75: 	\A                              # start of string
   76: 	\d{4}                           # 4-digit year
   77: 	- (?:0[1-9]|1[0-2])            # month 01-12
   78: 	- (?:0[1-9]|[12]\d|3[01])      # day   01-31
   79: 	[T\ ]                           # ISO 8601 date-time separator: T or space
   80: 	\d{2}:\d{2}                     # HH:MM -- no end anchor, see note above
   81: /x;
   82: my $INFER_DATE_RE = qr/
   83: 	\A                              # start of string
   84: 	\d{4}                           # 4-digit year
   85: 	- (?:0[1-9]|1[0-2])            # month 01-12
   86: 	- (?:0[1-9]|[12]\d|3[01])      # day   01-31
   87: 	\z                              # end of string
   88: /x;
   89: 
   90: # Module-level constant: valid JOIN types after uc() normalisation.
   91: # Built once at compile time; reused by every _build_joins call.
   92: my %VALID_JOIN_TYPES = map { $_ => 1 } qw(INNER LEFT RIGHT FULL CROSS);
   93: 
   94: # Valid aggregate functions (lower-cased) for _validate_aggregate_spec.
   95: my %VALID_AGG_FNS = map { $_ => 1 } qw(sum avg min max count count_distinct median stddev);
   96: 
   97: # Functions that are Perl-fold-only (no SQL pushdown).
   98: my %PERL_ONLY_AGG_FNS = map { $_ => 1 } qw(median stddev);

  100: =encoding utf8
  101: 
  102: =head1 NAME
  103: 
  104: Database::Abstraction - Read-only Database Abstraction Layer (ORM)
  105: 
  106: =head1 VERSION
  107: 
  108: Version 0.56
  109: 
  110: =cut
  111: 
  112: our $VERSION = '0.57';
  113: 
  114: =head1 DESCRIPTION
  115: 
  116: C<Database::Abstraction> is a read-only ORM for Perl that gives a uniform
  117: interface over CSV, PSV, TSV, JSON, XML, SQLite, DBM::Deep, BerkeleyDB, and
  118: Excel (XLS/XLSX) files - local, remote (via SSH), or fetched from a URL -
  119: without writing any SQL.
  120: Effectively it allows you to access a database table, of many different
  121: database formats, as an object.
  122: 
  123: Key features:
  124: 
  125: =over 4
  126: 
  127: =item *
  128: 
  129: B<No SQL required.>  Use plain Perl method calls for simple lookups and
  130: scans; switch storage formats without changing application code.
  131: 
  132: =item *
  133: 
  134: B<Rich query criteria.>  Pass plain values, SQL wildcards, C<undef> (IS NULL),
  135: comparison operators (C<< > >> C<< < >> C<< >= >> C<< <= >> C<!=>), pattern
  136: operators (C<-like>, C<-not_like>), set operators (C<-in>, C<-not_in>,
  137: C<-between>), and logical groupings (C<-or>, C<-and>).
  138: 
  139: =item *
  140: 
  141: B<Automatic joins.>  Add a C<join> parameter to any select method to
  142: combine tables with INNER, LEFT, RIGHT, FULL, or CROSS joins.
  143: 
  144: =item *
  145: 
  146: B<Chained query builder.>  The C<query()> method returns a
  147: L<Database::Abstraction::Query> object for fluent, composable queries:
  148: C<< $db->query->where(...)->order_by(...)->limit(...)->all() >>.
  149: 
  150: =item *
  151: 
  152: B<Aggregation.>  The C<aggregate()> method and the fluent
  153: C<< ->group_by()->measure()->having()->all() >> chain push C<GROUP BY> to
  154: SQL backends and fold rows in Perl on slurp-only backends.
  155: 
  156: =item *
  157: 
  158: B<Schema introspection.>  C<columns()> lists column names; C<schema()>
  159: returns full type/nullability metadata, using native driver introspection
  160: (C<PRAGMA table_info> for SQLite, C<column_info> for others).
  161: 
  162: =item *
  163: 
  164: B<DSN portability.>  Pass a C<dsn> (plus optional C<username>/C<password>)
  165: to connect to any DBI-supported database (SQLite, PostgreSQL, MySQL, ...)
  166: instead of pointing at a local file.
  167: 
  168: =item *
  169: 
  170: B<Performance.>  Small files are slurped into a RAM hash for sub-millisecond
  171: lookups.  All DBI statement handles are cached with C<prepare_cached()>.
  172: A CHI-compatible cache layer is also supported.
  173: 
  174: =back
  175: 
  176: =head1 SYNOPSIS
  177: 
  178:     # 1. Create a thin subclass for your table (e.g. Database/Foo.pm)
  179:     package Database::Foo;
  180:     use parent 'Database::Abstraction';
  181: 
  182:     # 2. Open the database - file is auto-detected from the class name
  183:     #    (looks for foo.sql / foo.sqlite / foo.sqlite3 / foo.psv / foo.tsv / foo.csv / foo.xlsx / foo.xml / foo.json / foo.db)
  184:     my $db = Database::Foo->new(directory => '/path/to/data');
  185: 
  186:     # 3. Simple lookups -----------------------------------------------
  187: 
  188:     # Fetch one row
  189:     my $row = $db->fetchrow_hashref(entry => 'key1');
  190: 
  191:     # Fetch all rows matching a criterion
  192:     my $rows = $db->selectall_arrayref(status => 'active');
  193: 
  194:     # Column shortcut via AUTOLOAD
  195:     my $name = $db->name(entry => 'key1');
  196: 
  197:     # 4. Rich criteria ------------------------------------------------
  198: 
  199:     # Comparison operators
  200:     my $high = $db->selectall_arrayref(score => { '>' => 90 });
  201: 
  202:     # Set membership
  203:     my $selected = $db->selectall_arrayref(
  204:         name => { -in => ['Alice', 'Bob'] }
  205:     );
  206: 
  207:     # Range
  208:     my $mid = $db->selectall_arrayref(
  209:         score => { -between => [60, 80] }
  210:     );
  211: 
  212:     # OR grouping
  213:     my $either = $db->selectall_arrayref(
  214:         -or => [
  215:             { status => 'active'    },
  216:             { score  => { '>' => 95 } },
  217:         ]
  218:     );
  219: 
  220:     # 5. Joins --------------------------------------------------------
  221: 
  222:     my $joined = $db->selectall_arrayref(
  223:         join => { table => 'dept', on => 'foo.dept_id = dept.id', type => 'LEFT' }
  224:     );
  225: 
  226:     # 6. Chained query builder ----------------------------------------
  227: 
  228:     my $results = $db->query
  229:         ->where(status => 'active')
  230:         ->where(score  => { '>=' => 80 })
  231:         ->order_by('score DESC')
  232:         ->limit(10)
  233:         ->all();
  234: 
  235:     my $first = $db->query->where(name => 'Alice')->first();
  236:     my $count = $db->query->where(status => 'active')->count();
  237: 
  238:     # 7. Connect via DSN (PostgreSQL, MySQL, SQLite, ...) ---------------
  239: 
  240:     my $db2 = Database::Foo->new(
  241:         dsn      => 'dbi:Pg:dbname=mydb;host=db.example.com',
  242:         username => 'myuser',
  243:         password => 's3cret',
  244:     );
  245: 
  246:     # 8. Schema introspection -----------------------------------------
  247: 
  248:     my $cols   = $db->columns();  # ['entry', 'name', 'score', ...]
  249:     my $schema = $db->schema();   # { name => { type=>'TEXT', nullable=>1, ... }, ... }
  250: 
  251:     # 9. Aggregation (GROUP BY) ----------------------------------------
  252: 
  253:     # Total revenue and order count per region
  254:     my $summary = $db->aggregate(
  255:         by       => ['region'],
  256:         measures => [
  257:             { col => 'amount', fn => 'sum',   as => 'revenue' },
  258:             {                  fn => 'count', as => 'orders'  },
  259:         ],
  260:     );
  261: 
  262:     # Grand total across all rows
  263:     my $totals = $db->aggregate(
  264:         measures => [{ col => 'amount', fn => 'sum', as => 'total' }],
  265:     )->[0];
  266: 
  267:     # Fluent style with HAVING and sort
  268:     my $top = $db->query
  269:         ->group_by('region')
  270:         ->measure('amount', 'sum',   'revenue')
  271:         ->measure(undef,    'count', 'orders')
  272:         ->having(revenue => { '>' => 1000 })
  273:         ->order_by('revenue DESC')
  274:         ->limit(5)
  275:         ->all();
  276: 
  277: =head1 QUICK START EXAMPLE
  278: 
  279: If F</var/dat/foo.csv> contains:
  280: 
  281:     "customer_id","name"
  282:     "plugh","John"
  283:     "xyzzy","Jane"
  284: 
  285: Create a driver in F<.../Database/foo.pm>:
  286: 
  287:     package Database::foo;
  288:     use parent 'Database::Abstraction';
  289: 
  290:     # Regular CSV: no entry column, comma-separated
  291:     sub new {
  292:         my ($class, %args) = @_;
  293:         return $class->SUPER::new(no_entry => 1, sep_char => ',', %args);
  294:     }
  295: 
  296: Then query it:
  297: 
  298:     my $foo = Database::foo->new(directory => '/var/dat');
  299: 
  300:     # Prints "John"
  301:     print 'Customer: ', $foo->name(customer_id => 'plugh'), "\n";
  302: 
  303:     # Returns { customer_id => 'xyzzy', name => 'Jane' }
  304:     my $row = $foo->fetchrow_hashref(customer_id => 'xyzzy');
  305: 
  306: =head1 FILE FORMATS
  307: 
  308: The module probes the C<directory> for files in this priority order:
  309: 
  310: =over 4
  311: 
  312: =item 1. C<SQLite>
  313: 
  314: File ending C<.sql>, C<.sqlite>, or C<.sqlite3>.
  315: Requires L<DBD::SQLite>.
  316: 
  317: =item 2. C<Deep>
  318: 
  319: DBM::Deep file ending C<.dbm> or C<.deep>.  The entire file is slurped
  320: into a plain Perl hash on open; all in-memory fast-paths apply.
  321: Requires L<DBM::Deep> (loaded lazily).
  322: 
  323: =item 3. C<PSV>
  324: 
  325: Pipe-separated file, ending C<.psv>.
  326: 
  327: =item 4. C<TSV>
  328: 
  329: Tab-separated file, ending C<.tsv>.
  330: 
  331: =item 5. C<CSV>
  332: 
  333: Comma (or custom) separated file, ending C<.csv> or C<.db>; can be
  334: gzipped (C<.csv.gz> or C<.db.gz>).
  335: B<Note:> the default separator is C<!> not C<,> for historical
  336: reasons - pass C<< sep_char => ',' >> for standard CSVs.
  337: Requires L<Text::xSV::Slurp> for the slurp fast-path (loaded lazily).
  338: 
  339: =item 6. C<Excel> (C<.xls>) and C<XLSX> (C<.xlsx>)
  340: 
  341: Two separate Excel backends - one per file format:
  342: 
  343: =over 4
  344: 
  345: =item B<.xls> - old binary format, opened via L<DBD::Excel> (which uses
  346: L<Spreadsheet::ParseExcel> internally).  All queries go through DBI/SQL;
  347: no in-memory slurp path.  C<max_slurp_size> has no effect.
  348: 
  349: =item B<.xlsx> - modern OOXML format, parsed directly via
  350: L<Spreadsheet::ParseXLSX> and slurped into an in-memory hash (keyed mode)
  351: or array (C<no_entry> mode).  No DBI handle is created; all queries use
  352: the in-memory fast-path.  Complex criteria (operator hashes, C<-or>/C<-and>)
  353: will fall through to the SQL path and croak - use simple scalar criteria.
  354: 
  355: =back
  356: 
  357: For both formats, each worksheet is a separate logical table; the active
  358: worksheet is determined by the class-derived table name (or the C<table>
  359: constructor parameter).  Both modules are loaded lazily.
  360: 
  361: =item 7. C<XML>
  362: 
  363: File ending C<.xml>.
  364: Requires L<XML::Simple> for the slurp fast-path (loaded lazily).
  365: 
  366: =item 8. C<JSON>
  367: 
  368: File ending C<.json>, slurped into memory via L<JSON::MaybeXS> (loaded
  369: lazily).
  370: 
  371: The file may contain either a JSON array of row objects:
  372: 
  373:     [
  374:       { "entry": "key1", "col": "val1" },
  375:       { "entry": "key2", "col": "val2" }
  376:     ]
  377: 
  378: or a JSON object whose keys are the primary-key values:
  379: 
  380:     {
  381:       "key1": { "col": "val1" },
  382:       "key2": { "col": "val2" }
  383:     }
  384: 
  385: In the object form, each key is injected into its row hash under the C<id>
  386: column name (default C<entry>), so all normal lookups work identically to
  387: the array form.
  388: 
  389: A zero-byte or whitespace-only file is treated as empty - all query methods
  390: return 0 / C<undef> / C<[]> without throwing.
  391: Requires L<JSON::MaybeXS> (loaded lazily).
  392: 
  393: =item 9. C<BerkeleyDB>
  394: 
  395: Binary key-value file ending C<.db>.
  396: 
  397: =item 10. C<HTML>
  398: 
  399: HTML page fetched via a C<url>.  Pass C<url => 'https://...'> instead of
  400: C<directory>; the module fetches the page with L<LWP::UserAgent::Cached>, parses all
  401: C<< <table> >> elements with L<HTML::TableExtract>, and slurps the first (or
  402: C<html_table_index>-selected) table into memory.  The first row of the table
  403: is treated as column headers.  Both modules are loaded lazily and are not
  404: required for other backends.
  405: 
  406: =back
  407: 
  408: Pass C<dsn> to bypass file detection entirely and connect via any DBI driver.
  409: Pass C<url> to fetch and slurp data from a remote source without a local
  410: directory.  When the URL returns C<Content-Type: application/json> or the URL
  411: path ends in C<.json>, the response is parsed as JSON (see item 8 above).
  412: Otherwise the response is parsed as an HTML page (item 10).
  413: 
  414: Example - fetching CPAN Testers results:
  415: 
  416:     package Database::cpantesters;
  417:     use parent 'Database::Abstraction';
  418: 
  419:     my $db = Database::cpantesters->new(
  420:         url      => 'https://www.cpantesters.org/show/Database-Abstraction.json',
  421:         no_entry => 1,
  422:     );
  423:     my $passes = $db->selectall_arrayref(grade => 'PASS');
  424: 
  425: =head1 QUERY CRITERIA
  426: 
  427: All select methods (C<selectall_arrayref>, C<selectall_array>,
  428: C<fetchrow_hashref>, C<count>) accept the same criteria syntax.
  429: 
  430: =head2 Plain value
  431: 
  432:     status => 'active'          # status = 'active'
  433:     name   => undef             # name IS NULL
  434: 
  435: Values containing C<%> or C<_> are matched with C<LIKE>:
  436: 
  437:     name => 'A%'                # name LIKE 'A%'
  438: 
  439: =head2 Comparison operator hashref
  440: 
  441:     score => { '>'  => 90  }   # score > 90
  442:     score => { '<'  => 50  }   # score < 50
  443:     score => { '>=' => 80  }   # score >= 80
  444:     score => { '<=' => 100 }   # score <= 100
  445:     score => { '!=' => 0   }   # score != 0
  446: 
  447: Multiple operators on one column are ANDed:
  448: 
  449:     score => { '>' => 60, '<' => 90 }   # 60 < score < 90
  450: 
  451: =head2 Pattern matching
  452: 
  453:     name => { -like     => 'A%'  }   # name LIKE 'A%'
  454:     name => { -not_like => 'Z%'  }   # name NOT LIKE 'Z%'
  455: 
  456: =head2 Set membership
  457: 
  458:     name => { -in     => ['Alice', 'Bob'] }   # name IN (...)
  459:     name => { -not_in => ['Alice', 'Bob'] }   # name NOT IN (...)
  460: 
  461: =head2 Range
  462: 
  463:     score => { -between => [60, 90] }   # score BETWEEN 60 AND 90
  464: 
  465: =head2 Logical groupings
  466: 
  467: C<-or> and C<-and> take an arrayref of condition hashrefs:
  468: 
  469:     -or => [
  470:         { status => 'active'        },
  471:         { score  => { '>' => 95 }   },
  472:     ]
  473: 
  474:     -and => [
  475:         { status => 'active'        },
  476:         { score  => { '>=' => 80 }  },
  477:     ]
  478: 
  479: =head2 Joins
  480: 
  481: Any select method accepts a C<join> key with a hashref (or arrayref of
  482: hashrefs) describing the join:
  483: 
  484:     join => {
  485:         table => 'dept',
  486:         on    => 'employees.dept_id = dept.id',   # raw SQL string
  487:         type  => 'LEFT',    # INNER (default) | LEFT | RIGHT | FULL | CROSS
  488:     }
  489: 
  490: The C<on> condition can also be expressed in Perl-native forms that avoid
  491: writing raw SQL:
  492: 
  493:     # Hashref: { left_col => right_col }  â€” equality, SQL-injection-safe
  494:     join => { table => 'dept', on => { 'employees.dept_id' => 'dept.id' } }
  495: 
  496:     # Multi-column equality join (hash pairs AND-ed together)
  497:     join => { table => 'dept', on => { 'e.dept_id' => 'd.id', 'e.region' => 'd.region' } }
  498: 
  499:     # Arrayref 2-element: [ left_col, right_col ]  â€” implies '='
  500:     join => { table => 'dept', on => [ 'employees.dept_id', 'dept.id' ] }
  501: 
  502:     # Arrayref 3-element: [ left_col, operator, right_col ]
  503:     # Valid operators: = != <> < > <= >=
  504:     join => { table => 'ranges', on => [ 'log.score', '>=', 'ranges.min_score' ] }
  505: 
  506:     # Multiple joins
  507:     join => [
  508:         { table => 'dept',    on => { 'e.dept_id'    => 'dept.id'    } },
  509:         { table => 'country', on => { 'e.country_id' => 'country.id' } },
  510:     ]
  511: 
  512: =head1 SUBROUTINES/METHODS
  513: 
  514: =head2 init
  515: 
  516: Set class-level defaults shared by all instances.
  517: 
  518:     Database::Abstraction::init(directory => '../data');
  519: 
  520: Accepts the same parameters as L</new>.  Returns a reference to the
  521: current defaults hash, so you can read them back:
  522: 
  523:     my $defaults = Database::Abstraction::init();
  524:     print $defaults->{'directory'}, "\n";
  525: 
  526: =cut
  527: 
  528: # Subroutine to initialize with args
  529: sub init
  530: {

  533: 			# Compatibility with CHI
  534: 			$params->{'cache_duration'} = $params->{'expires_in'};
  535: 		}
  536: 
  537: 		%defaults = (%defaults, %{$params});
  538: 		$defaults{'cache_duration'} ||= '1 hour';
  539: 	}

  571: 
  572: =head2 new
  573: 
  574: Create an object pointing to a read-only database.
  575: 
  576: Accepts arguments as a hash, a hashref, or - as a shortcut - a single bare
  577: string which is taken to be C<directory>.
  578: 
  579: =head3 Connection parameters
  580: 
  581: =over 4
  582: 
  583: =item * C<directory>
  584: 
  585: Directory containing the data files.  The module probes this directory for
  586: files named after the subclass (see L</FILE FORMATS>).  Required unless
  587: C<dsn> is given.
  588: 
  589: =item * C<dsn>
  590: 
  591: A DBI data-source string (e.g. C<dbi:SQLite:dbname=/path/to/db>,
  592: C<dbi:Pg:dbname=mydb;host=db.example.com>, or
  593: C<dbi:MariaDB:database=mydb;host=db.example.com>).  When present, file
  594: detection is skipped entirely and the DSN is used directly.  The SQL
  595: dialect is inferred from the DSN prefix (C<sqlite>, C<postgres>,
  596: C<mysql> â€” C<dbi:MariaDB:> maps to the C<mysql> dialect).
  597: 
  598: =item * C<username>
  599: 
  600: Database username.  Used only with C<dsn>; ignored for file-based backends.
  601: 
  602: =item * C<password>
  603: 
  604: Database password.  Used only with C<dsn>; ignored for file-based backends.
  605: 
  606: =item * C<dbname>
  607: 
  608: Override the filename stem searched in C<directory> (default: the table
  609: name derived from the class name).
  610: 
  611: =item * C<table>
  612: 
  613: Override the table (or worksheet) name used in SQL queries for this object.
  614: Default is the class-name-derived table name (e.g. C<Database::Foo> =>
  615: C<foo>).  Particularly useful for Excel workbooks where a single F<.xlsx>
  616: file contains multiple worksheets: pass C<< table => 'Summary' >> to query
  617: the C<Summary> worksheet without creating a dedicated subclass.  Also works
  618: with SQLite/DSN connections to select a table other than the class-derived
  619: default.  The filename stem (C<dbname>) continues to fall back to the class
  620: name, so the correct file is opened regardless of this override.  The value
  621: is validated against C<$SAFE_QUALIFIED> at construction time.
  622: 
  623: =item * C<filename>
  624: 
  625: Override the full filename (relative to C<directory>).  Takes precedence
  626: over C<dbname>.
  627: 
  628: =item * C<host>
  629: 
  630: Remote hostname (or C<user@host>) from which to fetch the data file(s) via
  631: SSH/SCP.  When present, each candidate filename is fetched with
  632: L<File::Slurp::Remote> into a local temporary directory; the existing
  633: extension-based file-type detection then runs against that directory.
  634: C<directory> is treated as the remote path (no local canonicalization is
  635: applied).  Using C<filename> together with C<host> avoids probing multiple
  636: extensions and is therefore more efficient.  L<File::Slurp::Remote> must be
  637: installed; it is loaded lazily (only when C<host> is given).
  638: 
  639: =item * C<url>
  640: 
  641: A URL (C<http://> or C<https://>) pointing to an HTML page that contains one
  642: or more C<< <table> >> elements.  When present, C<directory> is not required.
  643: The first row of the selected table is used as column headers.
  644: Requires L<LWP::UserAgent::Cached> and L<HTML::TableExtract> (both loaded lazily).
  645: 
  646: =item * C<database>
  647: 
  648: A Redis connection URL in the form C<redis://[password@]host[:port][/db_index]>.
  649: When present, C<directory>, C<dsn>, and C<url> are not required.  Rows are
  650: expected to be stored as Redis Hashes at keys of the form
  651: C<tablename:entry_value>; fields of each Hash become column values.  The
  652: module slurps all matching keys via C<KEYS tablename:*> + C<HGETALL> into the
  653: same in-memory structure used by the CSV and DBM::Deep backends, so all
  654: existing fast-paths (C<selectall_arrayref>, C<selectall_array>,
  655: C<fetchrow_hashref>, C<count>, C<columns>, C<schema>, AUTOLOAD) work without
  656: modification.  C<no_entry> mode is supported.
  657: 
  658: L<Redis::Fast> is tried first (XS, faster); if unavailable, L<Redis>
  659: (pure-Perl) is used as a fallback.  Both are loaded lazily - neither is
  660: required when using file-based or DSN-based backends.  The URL scheme is
  661: validated at construction time (C<redis://> only; all other schemes croak).
  662: 
  663: Use L</select> to switch the active Redis database on an already-open
  664: connection.
  665: 
  666: =back
  667: 
  668: =head3 Behaviour parameters
  669: 
  670: =over 4
  671: 
  672: =item * C<no_entry>
  673: 
  674: Set to C<1> when the table has no key column (standard CSVs, for example).
  675: Default is C<0> (keyed on C<entry>).
  676: 
  677: =item * C<id>
  678: 
  679: Name of the key column.  Default is C<entry>.
  680: 
  681: =item * C<sep_char>
  682: 
  683: Field separator for CSV/PSV files.
  684: Default is C<!> - pass C<< sep_char => ',' >>
  685: for standard comma-separated files.
  686: 
  687: A quote inside a quoted field may be written as two quotes
  688: (C<"a ""b"" c">, as RFC 4180 and spreadsheets write it) or with a backslash
  689: (C<"a \"b\" c">).  Each file is read with the one it uses: a backslash when
  690: the file holds a backslash before a quote anywhere, otherwise the quote.
  691: 
  692: =item * C<max_slurp_size>
  693: 
  694: Files smaller than this (in bytes) are loaded entirely into memory for fast
  695: lookups.  Default is 16 KB.  Set to C<0> to force SQL mode for all sizes.
  696: 
  697: =item * C<no_fixate>
  698: 
  699: Set to C<1> to return mutable arrays.  Default is C<0> (arrays are made
  700: read-only via L<Data::Reuse>).
  701: 
  702: =item * C<auto_load>
  703: 
  704: Set to C<0> to disable the AUTOLOAD column shortcut.  Default is C<1>
  705: (enabled).
  706: 
  707: =item * C<html_table_index>
  708: 
  709: Zero-based index of the HTML C<< <table> >> to extract when the C<url>
  710: backend is used.  Default is C<0> (the first table on the page).
  711: 
  712: =item * C<infer_types>
  713: 
  714: Set to C<1> to enable heuristic type inference in C<schema()> for
  715: slurp-backed sources (CSV, JSON, XLSX, XML, HTML URL, DBM::Deep).
  716: Default is C<0> (off; all columns reported as C<TEXT> for backward
  717: compatibility).
  718: 
  719: When enabled, C<schema()> scans the first 100 rows and promotes column
  720: types: all-integer values => C<INTEGER>; all-floating-point values =>
  721: C<REAL>; all ISO-8601 timestamps => C<TIMESTAMP>; all ISO-8601 dates =>
  722: C<DATE>; otherwise C<TEXT>.  C<NULL> (C<undef> or empty string) values
  723: are skipped during the scan.
  724: 
  725: =item * C<base_criteria>
  726: 
  727: A hashref of column-value pairs that is ANDed into B<every> SELECT
  728: automatically.  Callers do not need to repeat these criteria on each
  729: call.  Common use cases:
  730: 
  731: =over 4
  732: 
  733: =item * Row-level security: C<< base_criteria => { tenant_id => $tid } >>
  734: 
  735: =item * Soft-delete filtering: C<< base_criteria => { deleted_at => undef } >>
  736: 
  737: =item * Status gates: C<< base_criteria => { active => 1 } >>
  738: 
  739: =back
  740: 
  741: The hashref is shallow-copied at construction time so later mutations to
  742: the original do not affect the object.  Column names are validated
  743: against the same identifier rules as the C<id> parameter.  C<-or> and
  744: C<-and> groupings are allowed as keys.
  745: 
  746: B<SQL path>: the criteria are merged into the C<WHERE> clause before
  747: the caller-supplied criteria (caller values win on key collision).
  748: 
  749: B<Slurp/in-memory path>: the criteria are applied as an additional
  750: filter.  The C<fetchrow_hashref> entry-key fast path checks
  751: C<base_criteria> against the returned row before returning it.
  752: 
  753: B<Query builder>: C<base_criteria> are merged into the C<WHERE> clause
  754: generated by C<_build_sql>, so C<< $db->query->where(...)->all() >>
  755: also respects them.
  756: 
  757: =back
  758: 
  759: =head3 Caching and logging
  760: 
  761: =over 4
  762: 
  763: =item * C<cache>
  764: 
  765: A L<CHI>-compatible cache object.  When set, query results are stored and
  766: retrieved from the cache.
  767: 
  768: =item * C<cache_duration> / C<expires_in>
  769: 
  770: TTL for cached results.  Default is C<'1 hour'>.  C<expires_in> is a
  771: synonym for compatibility with L<CHI>.
  772: 
  773: =item * C<logger>
  774: 
  775: An object that understands C<warn()> and C<trace()> (e.g.
  776: L<Log::Log4perl>, L<Log::Any>), a code reference, or a filename.
  777: 
  778: =item * C<config_file>
  779: 
  780: Path to a YAML, XML, or INI configuration file whose keys are merged into
  781: the constructor arguments.  Loaded via L<Object::Configure>.
  782: 
  783: =back
  784: 
  785: =head3 Notes
  786: 
  787: =over 4
  788: 
  789: =item *
  790: 
  791: If no arguments are set, class-level defaults set via C<init()> or C<use>
  792: are used.
  793: 
  794: =item *
  795: 
  796: Slurp mode assumes the key column (C<entry>) is unique.  If it is not,
  797: searches will be incomplete - disable slurp mode by setting
  798: C<< max_slurp_size => 0 >>.
  799: 
  800: =item *
  801: 
  802: Passing an existing object as C<$class> clones it, merging any new
  803: arguments.
  804: 
  805: =back
  806: 
  807: =cut
  808:

 1740: 
 1741: =head2 selectall_arrayref
 1742: 
 1743: Returns a reference to an array of hash references for every row that
 1744: matches the given criteria, or C<undef> when there are no matches.
 1745: 
 1746:     my $rows = $db->selectall_arrayref();                    # all rows
 1747:     my $rows = $db->selectall_arrayref(status => 'active');  # exact match
 1748:     my $rows = $db->selectall_arrayref(score => { '>' => 8 });  # operator
 1749: 
 1750: The full criteria syntax is described in L</QUERY CRITERIA>.
 1751: 
 1752: Pass a C<join> key to combine with another table:
 1753: 
 1754:     my $rows = $db->selectall_arrayref(
 1755:         dept_name => 'Engineering',
 1756:         join      => { table => 'dept', on => 'e.dept_id = dept.id' },
 1757:     );
 1758: 
 1759: Pass C<limit =E<gt> N> and/or C<offset =E<gt> M> for pagination:
 1760: 
 1761:     my $page = $db->selectall_arrayref(status => 'active', limit => 10, offset => 20);
 1762: 
 1763: Both values must be non-negative integers; invalid values are ignored with a
 1764: C<carp> warning.  When C<offset> is given without C<limit> the SQL backend
 1765: uses C<LIMIT -1> on SQLite (meaning "no upper bound") so the C<OFFSET> clause
 1766: is legal.
 1767: 
 1768: Pass C<sort_by =E<gt> 'col'> (ascending) or C<sort_by =E<gt> ['col', 'DESC']>
 1769: to request a specific sort column and direction instead of the default primary-key
 1770: ordering:
 1771: 
 1772:     my $rows = $db->selectall_arrayref(sort_by => 'name');
 1773:     my $rows = $db->selectall_arrayref(sort_by => ['score', 'DESC']);
 1774: 
 1775: The column name is validated against the same identifier rules as all other
 1776: column parameters.  An unsafe name or an unrecognised direction (anything other
 1777: than C<ASC> or C<DESC>, case-insensitive) is ignored with a C<carp> warning and
 1778: the default sort order is used instead.  Sorting is applied before
 1779: C<limit>/C<offset> pagination.
 1780: 
 1781: Pass C<distinct =E<gt> 1> to return only unique rows (deduplication):
 1782: 
 1783:     my $rows = $db->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1);
 1784:     my $rows = $db->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1, columns => [qw(status dept)]);
 1785: 
 1786: Pass C<columns =E<gt> \@cols> to restrict which columns appear in the returned
 1787: rows.  Column names are validated against the same identifier rules as other
 1788: parameters; invalid names are dropped with a C<carp> warning.
 1789: 
 1790: On SQL-capable backends (SQLite, PostgreSQL, MySQL, DBD::CSV E<ge> 0.64)
 1791: C<distinct =E<gt> 1> emits C<SELECT DISTINCT>.  On the slurp (in-memory) path
 1792: C<distinct> without C<columns> is a no-op because each row is already unique.
 1793: 
 1794: B<Order of operations:> sort E<rarr> offset E<rarr> limit E<rarr> C<_apply_distinct>.
 1795: This means C<< limit => N, distinct => 1 >> deduplicates the already-limited
 1796: slice, not the full result set.  If you need N distinct rows from a larger set,
 1797: omit C<limit> and slice the returned arrayref yourself.
 1798: 
 1799: Results are returned in the cache (if configured) and the returned array
 1800: reference is made read-only unless C<no_fixate> was set.
 1801: 
 1802: B<Note:> this always returns all matching rows.  Use L</selectall_array>
 1803: in scalar context, or C<< $db->query->limit(1)->all() >>, to fetch just one row.
 1804: 
 1805: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
 1806: 
 1807:     1. Parse criteria; extract and build any JOIN clause.
 1808:        Extract distinct, columns, limit, offset, sort_by.
 1809:     2. If data is slurped AND no joins AND criteria are simple:
 1810:        a. No criteria -> return all rows as arrayref.
 1811:        b. entry-only lookup -> return [$data{entry}].
 1812:        c. Otherwise -> scan rows in-memory with _match_criterion.
 1813:        In all slurp cases: sort by sort_by column (if given), then apply
 1814:        offset/limit, then _apply_distinct (columns projection + dedup).
 1815:     3. Otherwise build SQL: SELECT [DISTINCT] [columns|*] FROM table
 1816:        [JOIN] [WHERE] ORDER BY sort_by [else id] [LIMIT] [OFFSET].
 1817:        All DBI backends (including DBD::CSV >= 0.64) use native SELECT DISTINCT.
 1818:     4. Check cache; return cached arrayref on HIT.
 1819:     5. prepare_cached + execute; fetch all rows.
 1820:     6. Store result in cache; fixate the array; return arrayref.
 1821: 
 1822: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 1823: 
 1824: =head4 Arguments
 1825: 
 1826:     %criteria   # optional key-value pairs or operator hashrefs
 1827:     join        => HashRef | ArrayRef  # optional join spec(s)
 1828:     sort_by     => Str | ArrayRef      # optional: 'col' or ['col','DESC']
 1829:     limit       => Int                 # optional: max rows
 1830:     offset      => Int                 # optional: rows to skip
 1831:     distinct    => Bool                # optional: deduplicate rows
 1832:     columns     => ArrayRef[Str]       # optional: restrict output columns
 1833:     no_fixate   => Bool                # optional: skip read-only locking
 1834:     table       => Str                 # optional: override table name
 1835: 
 1836: =head4 Returns
 1837: 
 1838:     ArrayRef[HashRef] | undef
 1839: 
 1840: An arrayref of row hashrefs on success.  Returns C<undef> when no
 1841: rows match.  Each hashref is read-only unless C<no_fixate> was set.
 1842: 
 1843: =cut
 1844:

 2154: 
 2155: =head2 each_row
 2156: 
 2157:     $db->each_row(\&callback);
 2158:     $db->each_row(\&callback, status => 'active');
 2159:     $db->each_row(\&callback, sort_by => 'name', limit => 100, offset => 20);
 2160: 
 2161: Iterates over matching rows one at a time, calling C<\&callback> once per
 2162: row with the row hashref as the sole argument.  Uses B<constant memory> on
 2163: the SQL path: rows are fetched from the database one at a time via
 2164: C<fetchrow_hashref> without materialising the full result array.  On the
 2165: slurp/in-memory path data is already in RAM, so memory use is equivalent to
 2166: L</selectall_arrayref>.
 2167: 
 2168: Accepts the same criteria, C<join>, C<sort_by>, C<limit>, and C<offset>
 2169: parameters as L</selectall_arrayref>.
 2170: 
 2171: Returns the number of rows passed to C<\&callback>.
 2172: 
 2173: Exceptions raised inside C<\&callback> abort iteration and propagate to
 2174: the caller; the DBI statement handle is left in a valid state (C<finish()>
 2175: is called on exception).
 2176: 
 2177: B<Note:> rows on the SQL path are not fixated (made read-only) because they
 2178: are discarded after each callback invocation.  Slurp-path rows are already
 2179: fixated from the initial load.
 2180: 
 2181: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2182: 
 2183: =head4 Arguments
 2184: 
 2185:     CodeRef     # required: callback receiving one row hashref per call
 2186:     %criteria   # optional: same criteria syntax as selectall_arrayref
 2187:     join        => HashRef | ArrayRef  # optional join spec(s)
 2188:     sort_by     => Str | ArrayRef      # optional sort column
 2189:     limit       => Int                 # optional: max rows to visit
 2190:     offset      => Int                 # optional: rows to skip
 2191: 
 2192: =head4 Returns
 2193: 
 2194:     Int (>= 0)
 2195: 
 2196: The number of rows visited (i.e. the number of times the callback
 2197: was called).  Any exception thrown inside the callback is re-thrown
 2198: after the DBI statement handle is properly finished.
 2199: 
 2200: =cut
 2201:

 2388: =head2 selectall_array
 2389: 
 2390: Similar to L</selectall_arrayref> but returns a list of hash references
 2391: rather than a reference to an array.
 2392: 
 2393:     my @rows = $db->selectall_array(status => 'active');
 2394: 
 2395: In B<scalar context> it applies C<LIMIT 1> and returns just the first
 2396: matching hash reference - making it more efficient than C<selectall_arrayref>
 2397: when you only need one row.  In B<list context> all matching rows are returned.
 2398: 
 2399: Accepts the same criteria, C<join>, C<limit>, C<offset>, and C<sort_by>
 2400: parameters as L</selectall_arrayref>.  When C<limit> is given in scalar context
 2401: it overrides the implicit C<LIMIT 1>.
 2402: 
 2403: =cut

 2791: 
 2792: =head2 fetchrow_hashref
 2793: 
 2794: Returns a hash reference for the first row matching the given criteria,
 2795: or C<undef> when there is no match.  Always applies C<LIMIT 1>.
 2796: 
 2797:     my $row = $db->fetchrow_hashref(entry => 'key1');
 2798:     my $row = $db->fetchrow_hashref(score => { '>=' => 10 });
 2799: 
 2800: When C<no_entry> is B<not> set you may pass a single bare value and it is
 2801: used as the C<entry> key:
 2802: 
 2803:     my $row = $db->fetchrow_hashref('key1');    # same as entry => 'key1'
 2804: 
 2805: Accepts the full criteria syntax described in L</QUERY CRITERIA>, including
 2806: the C<join> parameter:
 2807: 
 2808:     my $row = $db->fetchrow_hashref(
 2809:         name => 'Alice',
 2810:         join => { table => 'dept', on => 'e.dept_id = dept.id' },
 2811:     );
 2812: 
 2813: Pass C<< table => $other_table >> to query a table other than the one
 2814: derived from the class name.
 2815: 
 2816: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2817: 
 2818: =head4 Arguments
 2819: 
 2820:     entry       => Str    # primary-key value to look up (positional first
 2821:                           # arg when no_entry is false)
 2822:     %criteria   # optional additional column filters
 2823:     join        => HashRef | ArrayRef  # optional join spec(s)
 2824:     no_fixate   => Bool                # optional: skip read-only locking
 2825:     table       => Str                 # optional: override table name
 2826: 
 2827: =head4 Returns
 2828: 
 2829:     HashRef | undef
 2830: 
 2831: A single row hashref when exactly one matching row is found.  Returns
 2832: C<undef> when the key is not found or no row matches the criteria.
 2833: The hashref is read-only unless C<no_fixate> was set.
 2834: 
 2835: B<Note:> C<fetchrow_hashref> does B<not> croak when multiple rows
 2836: match the given criteria; it returns whichever row the backend
 2837: delivers first (usually the first row in file/insertion order).
 2838: 
 2839: =cut
 2840:

 2955: =head2 execute
 2956: 
 2957: Execute a raw SQL query on the underlying database.
 2958: 
 2959:     # Scalar context: returns the first row as a hashref
 2960:     my $row = $db->execute(query => 'SELECT * FROM foo WHERE id = 1');
 2961: 
 2962:     # List context: returns all rows as a list of hashrefs
 2963:     my @rows = $db->execute(query => 'SELECT * FROM foo WHERE score > ?',
 2964:                             args  => [80]);

 2967: 
 2968: On CSV tables without C<no_entry> it may help to add
 2969: C<WHERE entry IS NOT NULL AND entry NOT LIKE '#%'> to filter comment rows.

 2971: If the data have been slurped into memory this method still hits the actual
 2972: database file directly.
 2973:

 3029: =head2 updated
 3030: 
 3031: Returns the Unix timestamp of the last database update.
 3032: 
 3033: For file-based backends (CSV, XML, SQLite via C<directory>), this is the
 3034: mtime of the backing file, set at C<new()> time.
 3035: 
 3036: For SQLite DSN connections (C<dbi:SQLite:dbname=...>), the file path is
 3037: extracted from the DSN and C<stat()>-ed live on every call, so callers
 3038: get a current mtime suitable for cache-invalidation even when the database
 3039: was opened via a DSN rather than a C<directory>.
 3040: 
 3041: For all other DSN-based connections (PostgreSQL, MySQL, etc.) and for
 3042: URL-based backends, returns the Unix timestamp of the most recent

 3062: =head2 columns
 3063: 
 3064: Returns an array reference of column names for the current table.
 3065: 
 3066:     my $cols = $db->columns();    # e.g. ['entry', 'name', 'score', 'status']
 3067: 
 3068: Column names are always returned in alphabetical (lexicographic) order,
 3069: regardless of backend. This makes the result stable and portable when the
 3070: same logical table is backed by different engines (CSV => SQLite, etc.).
 3071: 
 3072: The source of column names varies by backend:
 3073: 
 3074: =over 4
 3075: 
 3076: =item * B<Slurp mode> - keys of the first row in memory.
 3077: 
 3078: =item * B<SQLite / other DBI> - a zero-row C<SELECT *> exposes the driver's
 3079: C<NAME> attribute.
 3080: 
 3081: =item * B<BerkeleyDB> - always returns C<['entry', 'value']>.
 3082: 
 3083: =back
 3084: 
 3085: The result is cached inside the object after the first call.
 3086: 
 3087: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3088: 
 3089: =head4 Arguments
 3090: 
 3091: None beyond the implicit invocant.  The result is cached in the
 3092: object after the first call.
 3093: 
 3094: =head4 Returns
 3095: 
 3096:     ArrayRef[Str]
 3097: 
 3098: A sorted (lexicographic, ascending) arrayref of column names.  The
 3099: sort order is identical across all backends, so switching from CSV
 3100: to SQLite does not change the returned order.
 3101: 
 3102: =cut

 3134: =head2 schema
 3135: 
 3136: Returns a hash reference describing the schema of the current table.
 3137: Each key is a column name; each value is a hash reference with these keys:
 3138: 
 3139: =over 4
 3140: 
 3141: =item * C<type> - data type string (e.g. C<TEXT>, C<INTEGER>, C<REAL>)
 3142: 
 3143: =item * C<nullable> - C<1> if the column may be NULL, C<0> if NOT NULL
 3144: 
 3145: =item * C<default> - default value string, or C<undef>
 3146: 
 3147: =item * C<pk> - C<1> if this column is (part of) the primary key, C<0> otherwise
 3148: 
 3149: =back
 3150: 
 3151:     my $schema = $db->schema();
 3152: 
 3153:     for my $col (sort keys %{$schema}) {
 3154:         my $info = $schema->{$col};
 3155:         printf "%s  %s  %s\n",
 3156:             $col,
 3157:             $info->{type},
 3158:             $info->{nullable} ? 'NULL' : 'NOT NULL';
 3159:     }
 3160: 
 3161: The schema is determined by the backend:
 3162: 
 3163: =over 4
 3164: 
 3165: =item * B<SQLite> - C<PRAGMA table_info(table)>
 3166: 
 3167: =item * B<Other DBI drivers> - C<< $dbh->column_info(...) >>
 3168: 
 3169: =item * B<Slurp mode> - inferred from the first row; all columns typed as
 3170: C<TEXT> by default.  When C<< infer_types => 1 >> was passed to the
 3171: constructor, up to 100 rows are scanned per column and the type is
 3172: promoted: all-integer values => C<INTEGER>; all-float values =>
 3173: C<REAL>; all ISO-8601 timestamps (C<YYYY-MM-DDThh:mm>) => C<TIMESTAMP>;
 3174: all ISO-8601 dates (C<YYYY-MM-DD>) => C<DATE>; otherwise C<TEXT>.
 3175: C<undef> and empty-string values are treated as SQL C<NULL> and skipped
 3176: during the scan.
 3177: 
 3178: =item * B<BerkeleyDB> - always returns C<entry> (pk) and C<value>
 3179: 
 3180: =back
 3181: 
 3182: The result is cached inside the object after the first call.
 3183: 
 3184: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3185: 
 3186: =head4 Arguments
 3187: 
 3188:     infer_types => Bool  # enable heuristic type inference for slurp backends
 3189:                          # (may also be set at construction time)
 3190: 
 3191: =head4 Returns
 3192: 
 3193:     HashRef[Str => HashRef]
 3194: 
 3195: A hashref keyed by column name.  Each value is a hashref with:
 3196: 
 3197:     type     => Str          # 'TEXT', 'INTEGER', 'REAL', 'TIMESTAMP', 'DATE'
 3198:     nullable => Bool         # 1 if the column may be NULL, 0 if NOT NULL
 3199:     default  => Str | undef  # default value, or undef
 3200:     pk       => Bool         # 1 if (part of) the primary key
 3201: 
 3202: For slurp-mode backends all columns default to type C<TEXT> unless
 3203: C<infer_types> is set.  The result is cached in the object after the
 3204: first call.
 3205: 
 3206: =cut

 3289: =head2 dbi_source
 3290: 
 3291: Returns a hashref C<{ dbh =E<gt> $dbh, table =E<gt> $name }> when the backend
 3292: is a live SQLite connection, or C<undef> for every other backend (slurp-mode
 3293: CSV, JSON, XLSX, HTML URL, DBM::Deep, BerkeleyDB, PostgreSQL, MySQL, ...).
 3294: 
 3295: The hashref is consumed by C<Database::Join> to perform a zero-copy
 3296: C<ATTACH DATABASE> so rows never pass through Perl.
 3297: Nested C<Database::Join> objects that are themselves SQLite-backed expose themselves
 3298: as attachable sources to parent joins through the same interface.
 3299: 
 3300: Subclasses may override this method to expose non-SQLite DBI connections
 3301: if their join layer supports them.
 3302: 
 3303: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3304: 
 3305: =head4 Arguments
 3306: 
 3307: None beyond the implicit invocant.
 3308: 
 3309: =head4 Returns
 3310: 
 3311: A hashref C<{ dbh =E<gt> DBI::db, table =E<gt> Str }> on a SQLite-backed
 3312: instance, or C<undef> on all other backends.
 3313: 
 3314: =cut
 3315: 
 3316: sub dbi_source
 3317: {
 3318: 	my $self = shift;
 3319: 
 3320: 	my $table = $self->_open_table({});
 3321: 	my $dbh   = $self->{'_dbh'}{$table};
 3322:

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 3324: 
 3325: 	return { dbh => $dbh, table => $table };
 3326: }
 3327: 
 3328: =head2 aggregate
 3329: 
 3330: Groups matching rows and computes aggregate measures over each group.
 3331: Pushes C<GROUP BY> SQL to the backend when a DBI handle is available;
 3332: otherwise folds rows in Perl (BerkeleyDB, DBM::Deep, XLSX, JSON, HTML, Redis).
 3333: 
 3334:     my $rows = $db->aggregate(
 3335:         by       => ['region', 'month'],
 3336:         measures => [
 3337:             { col => 'amount', fn => 'sum',   as => 'revenue' },
 3338:             {                  fn => 'count', as => 'orders'  },
 3339:         ],
 3340:         status => 'active',
 3341:     );
 3342: 
 3343:     # Grand total (no 'by')
 3344:     my $row = $db->aggregate(
 3345:         measures => [{ col => 'score', fn => 'avg', as => 'mean_score' }],
 3346:     )->[0];
 3347: 
 3348: Reserved parameter keys (extracted before the rest is treated as WHERE criteria):
 3349: C<by>, C<measures>, C<having>, C<sort_by>, C<limit>, C<offset>, C<table>, C<join>.
 3350: 
 3351: Always returns an B<arrayref of hashrefs> (never C<undef>); returns C<[]> when
 3352: no group qualifies.  Row keys are the C<by> column names (in declaration order)
 3353: followed by the measure aliases.
 3354: 
 3355: =head3 Measure specification
 3356: 
 3357: Each measure hashref:
 3358: 
 3359:     { col => 'amount', fn => 'sum',           as => 'revenue'   }
 3360:     {                  fn => 'count',         as => 'orders'    }  # COUNT(*)
 3361:     { col => 'amount', fn => 'count',         as => 'paid'      }  # COUNT(col)
 3362:     { col => 'customer', fn => 'count_distinct', as => 'customers' }
 3363: 
 3364: C<fn> must be one of C<sum>, C<avg>, C<min>, C<max>, C<count>, C<count_distinct>,
 3365: C<median>, C<stddev> (case-insensitive).  C<col> is required for every function
 3366: except plain C<count>.  C<as> (the result key) must be a valid SQL identifier
 3367: and must not duplicate another alias or a C<by> column name.
 3368: 
 3369: C<median> computes the median (middle value of sorted values; average of two
 3370: middle values for even count).  C<stddev> computes the sample standard deviation
 3371: (using N-1 denominator).  Both are computed in Perl (no SQL pushdown); on SQL
 3372: backends the full result set is fetched first and then folded in memory.
 3373: 
 3374: Empty-set normalisation: C<median> and C<stddev> become C<undef> when no rows
 3375: are present; C<stddev> on a single-row group returns C<0>.
 3376: 
 3377: =head3 HAVING
 3378: 
 3379: Pass C<< having => { alias_or_by_col => criterion } >> to filter groups
 3380: after aggregation.  Accepts the full criteria syntax.
 3381: 
 3382:     having => { revenue => { '>' => 1000 } }
 3383: 
 3384: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
 3385: 
 3386:     1. _open_table; croak if JOIN requested on no-DBI backend.
 3387:     2. _validate_aggregate_spec: extract/validate by/measures/having/
 3388:        sort_by/limit/offset; remainder of params is WHERE criteria.
 3389:     3. Merge base_criteria.
 3390:     4. SQL path (DBI handle): _build_aggregate_sql; prepare_cached+execute;
 3391:        normalise empty-set values; cache; fixate; return.
 3392:     5. Perl fold: selectall_arrayref(criteria); _fold_aggregate;
 3393:        HAVING via _match_criteria; sort; offset; limit; fixate; return.
 3394: 
 3395: =head3 Backend notes
 3396: 
 3397: On slurp-only backends all matching rows are materialised before folding.
 3398: Use a SQL backend (SQLite DSN, PostgreSQL, MySQL) for large datasets.
 3399: 
 3400: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3401: 
 3402: =head4 Arguments
 3403: 
 3404:     by         => ArrayRef[Str]     # optional: GROUP BY column names
 3405:     measures   => ArrayRef[HashRef] # required: one or more measure specs
 3406:     having     => HashRef           # optional: post-group filter criteria
 3407:     sort_by    => Str | ArrayRef    # optional: sort column (Str) or [col,dir]
 3408:     limit      => Int               # optional: max rows to return
 3409:     offset     => Int               # optional: rows to skip before returning
 3410:     join       => HashRef | ArrayRef # optional: join spec(s) -- SQL path only
 3411:     no_fixate  => Bool              # optional: skip read-only locking
 3412:     %criteria                       # optional: WHERE filter before grouping
 3413: 
 3414: Each element of C<measures> is a hashref with keys:
 3415: 
 3416:     col  => Str    # column to aggregate; omit only for plain count()
 3417:     fn   => Str    # one of: sum avg min max count count_distinct median stddev
 3418:     as   => Str    # result key name (required; valid SQL identifier)
 3419: 
 3420: =head4 Returns
 3421: 
 3422:     ArrayRef[HashRef]
 3423: 
 3424: An arrayref of hashrefs.  Each hashref has one key per C<by> column
 3425: and one key per measure C<as> alias.  Returns C<[]> (empty arrayref)
 3426: when no groups match -- never C<undef>.
 3427: 
 3428: Empty-set normalisation: C<sum>/C<count>/C<count_distinct> become
 3429: C<0>; C<avg>/C<min>/C<max>/C<median>/C<stddev> become C<undef> when no rows
 3430: are present.
 3431: 
 3432: =cut
 3433:

 3761: # Build a HAVING clause fragment, substituting measure aliases with their SQL
 3762: # expressions (PostgreSQL forbids output-column aliases in HAVING).
 3763: # $alias_to_sql maps alias â†’ SQL expression (e.g. revenue â†’ SUM(amount)).
 3764: # Mirrors _build_where_conditions but uses pre-validated SQL expressions as LHS
 3765: # for measure keys, so the $SAFE_QUALIFIED identifier check is skipped there.
 3766: #
 3767: # SQLite 3.51+ regression: bound parameters compared to aggregate expressions
 3768: # (SUM, AVG, etc.) in HAVING are not type-coerced to numeric automatically,
 3769: # causing numeric comparisons to return 0 rows.  When the LHS was substituted
 3770: # from an alias (i.e. it is an aggregate expression, not a bare column name),
 3771: # wrap all numeric bind placeholders in CAST(? AS REAL) so SQLite performs the
 3772: # comparison correctly.  CAST(? AS REAL) is valid SQL in SQLite, PostgreSQL and
 3773: # MySQL and is a no-op on those dialects where type coercion already works.
 3774: #
 3775: # Returns ($fragment, \@bind_values).
 3776: sub _build_having_fragment
 3777: {
●3778 → 3781 → 3834 3778: 	my ($self, $having, $alias_to_sql) = @_;

 3784: 		my $val      = $having->{$k};

 3787: 		# expression LHS (numeric result), bare ? for group-by column LHS.
 3788: 		my $ph = defined($agg_expr) ? 'CAST(? AS REAL)' : '?';
 3789:

 4086: =head2 AUTOLOAD - column shortcut
 4087: 
 4088: Calling an unknown method whose name matches a column name performs a column
 4089: lookup.  The method name is the column you want; the arguments are criteria.
 4090: 
 4091:     # Scalar context: return the first match
 4092:     my $name = $db->name(entry => 'key1');
 4093: 
 4094:     # List context: return all matching values
 4095:     my @names = $db->name();
 4096: 
 4097:     # Shortcut when the table has an 'entry' key column
 4098:     my $name = $db->name('key1');    # same as name(entry => 'key1')
 4099: 
 4100:     # Unique/distinct values
 4101:     my @statuses = $db->status(distinct => 1);
 4102: 
 4103: B<In list context> the full column is returned (all rows), ordered by the
 4104: column value.  B<In scalar context> only the first match is returned
 4105: (C<LIMIT 1>).
 4106: 
 4107: Results come from the slurp cache when available.
 4108: 
 4109: Throws an error if the column does not exist (slurp mode) or if AUTOLOAD
 4110: has been disabled with C<< auto_load => 0 >>.
 4111: 
 4112: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
 4113: 
 4114:     1. Extract column name from $AUTOLOAD; guard on DESTROY.
 4115:     2. Croak if auto_load => 0.
 4116:     3. Validate $column against /^[a-zA-Z_][a-zA-Z0-9_]*$/.
 4117:     4. If data is slurped:
 4118:        a. List context, no params -> map column over all rows (exists guard).
 4119:        b. entry-only param -> direct hash lookup (exists guard).
 4120:        c. No params, scalar -> first value in hash.
 4121:        d. no_entry set -> scan array for matching key/value pair.
 4122:        e. Other params -> scan keyed hash for matching column.
 4123:     5. If not slurped, build SQL:
 4124:        - List:   SELECT column FROM table [WHERE ...] ORDER BY column
 4125:        - Scalar: SELECT DISTINCT column FROM table [WHERE ...] LIMIT 1
 4126:     6. Check cache; return on HIT.
 4127:     7. prepare_cached + execute; fetch result.
 4128:     8. Store in cache; fixate; return.
 4129: 
 4130: =cut
 4131:

 4926: # The escape character a CSV/PSV/TSV file is written with.  RFC 4180 (and
 4927: # every spreadsheet) writes a quote inside a quoted field as two quotes,

 4929: # other files write it \", which a quote escape cannot read at all.  So: a
 4930: # backslash before a quote anywhere means backslash, the escape this module

 5097: =head1 MESSAGES
 5098: 
 5099: The table below lists every error that the module can croak or carp, what
 5100: triggers it, and how to resolve it.
 5101: 
 5102: =over 4
 5103: 
 5104: =item C<< I<Class>: abstract class >>
 5105: 
 5106: Direct instantiation of C<Database::Abstraction> was attempted.
 5107: Create a subclass and instantiate that instead.
 5108: 
 5109: =item C<< I<Class>: where are the files? >>
 5110: 
 5111: Neither C<directory> nor C<dsn> was supplied to C<new()>.
 5112: 
 5113: =item C<< I<Class>: I</path> is not a directory >>
 5114: 
 5115: The C<directory> argument exists on disk but is not a directory.
 5116: 
 5117: =item C<< I<Class>: cannot connect: I<$DBI::errstr> >>
 5118: 
 5119: DBI failed to connect to the given C<dsn>.  Check credentials and host.
 5120: 
 5121: =item C<< Can't find a file called 'I<name>' for the table I<T> in I<dir> >>
 5122: 
 5123: None of the probe extensions (C<.sql>, C<.sqlite>, C<.sqlite3>, C<.psv>, C<.tsv>, C<.csv>, C<.xlsx>, C<.db>, C<.xml>)
 5124: matched in C<directory>.
 5125: 
 5126: =item C<< I<Class>: prepare failed: I<$errstr> >>
 5127: 
 5128: C<prepare_cached()> returned false.  Usually a syntax error in an internally
 5129: built query; file a bug if you see this from a normal API call.
 5130: 
 5131: =item C<< _build_where_conditions: unsafe column name 'I<name>' >>
 5132: 
 5133: A criteria key contained characters outside C<[A-Za-z0-9_.]>.
 5134: This is a SQL-injection guard.  Use only valid SQL identifier characters.
 5135: 
 5136: =item C<< join: missing "table" >> / C<< join: missing "on" condition >>
 5137: 
 5138: A join spec hashref is incomplete.  Both C<table> and C<on> are required.
 5139: 
 5140: =item C<< Invalid JOIN type: I<TYPE> >>
 5141: 
 5142: C<type> in a join spec was not one of C<INNER LEFT RIGHT FULL CROSS>.
 5143: 
 5144: =item C<< I<Class>: Unknown column I<col> >> / C<< I<Class>: AUTOLOAD disabled >>
 5145: 
 5146: An AUTOLOAD call was made for a column that does not exist, or AUTOLOAD
 5147: was disabled with C<< auto_load => 0 >>.
 5148: 
 5149: =item C<< Usage: set_logger(logger => $logger) >>
 5150: 
 5151: C<set_logger()> was called without a C<logger> argument.
 5152: 
 5153: =item C<< Usage: execute(query => $query) >>
 5154: 
 5155: C<execute()> was called without a C<query> argument.
 5156: 
 5157: =item C<< XML slurp: I<...> is not yet supported >>
 5158: 
 5159: The XML file structure is too complex for slurp mode.
 5160: Use C<< max_slurp_size => 0 >> to force the DBI/XMLSimple SQL path.
 5161: 
 5162: =item C<< I<Class>: I<method> is meaningless on a NoSQL database >>
 5163: 
 5164: A relational method (C<selectall_arrayref>, C<count>, C<execute>, etc.)
 5165: was called on a BerkeleyDB backend, which only supports key-value lookup
 5166: via C<fetchrow_hashref>.
 5167: 
 5168: =item C<< I<Class>: aggregate: 'measures' must be a non-empty arrayref >>
 5169: 
 5170: C<aggregate()> was called without a C<measures> arrayref, or with an empty one.
 5171: 
 5172: =item C<< I<Class>: aggregate: measure must be a hashref, got I<ref> >>
 5173: 
 5174: An element of the C<measures> arrayref is not a hashref.
 5175: 
 5176: =item C<< I<Class>: aggregate: unknown measure function 'I<fn>' >>
 5177: 
 5178: C<fn> must be one of C<sum>, C<avg>, C<min>, C<max>, C<count>, C<count_distinct>,
 5179: C<median>, C<stddev>.
 5180: 
 5181: =item C<< I<Class>: aggregate: measure column is required for function 'I<fn>' >>
 5182: 
 5183: C<col> was omitted for a function that requires it.  Only plain C<count> (COUNT(*)) accepts a missing C<col>.
 5184: 
 5185: =item C<< I<Class>: aggregate: unsafe measure column 'I<col>' >>
 5186: 
 5187: The C<col> value is not a valid SQL identifier.
 5188: 
 5189: =item C<< I<Class>: aggregate: measure 'as' is required >>
 5190: 
 5191: The C<as> alias was missing from a measure hashref.
 5192: 
 5193: =item C<< I<Class>: aggregate: unsafe measure alias 'I<as>' >>
 5194: 
 5195: The C<as> value is not a valid SQL identifier.
 5196: 
 5197: =item C<< I<Class>: aggregate: duplicate measure alias 'I<as>' >>
 5198: 
 5199: Two measure hashrefs share the same C<as> alias.
 5200: 
 5201: =item C<< I<Class>: aggregate: measure alias 'I<as>' collides with a group-by column >>
 5202: 
 5203: A measure alias duplicates a name in the C<by> list.
 5204: 
 5205: =item C<< I<Class>: aggregate: 'by' must be an arrayref of column names >>
 5206: 
 5207: The C<by> parameter was not an arrayref.
 5208: 
 5209: =item C<< I<Class>: aggregate: unsafe group-by column 'I<col>' >>
 5210: 
 5211: A C<by> element is not a valid SQL identifier.
 5212: 
 5213: =item C<< I<Class>: aggregate: having key 'I<key>' is not a group-by column or measure alias >>
 5214: 
 5215: A C<having> key does not match any C<by> column name or measure C<as> alias.
 5216: 
 5217: =item C<< I<Class>: aggregate: sort_by names 'I<name>', which is not a group-by column or measure alias >>
 5218: 
 5219: The C<sort_by> column is not one of the C<by> columns or measure aliases.
 5220: 
 5221: =item C<< I<Class>: aggregate: cannot aggregate a JOIN on the I<backend> backend >>
 5222: 
 5223: C<join> was passed to C<aggregate()> on a slurp-only backend that has no DBI handle.
 5224: 
 5225: =item C<< Database::Abstraction::Query: count() is not available once measure() has been called >>
 5226: 
 5227: C<count()> was called on a query builder that has measures set.  Use C<all()> and check C<scalar @{$rows}>.
 5228: 
 5229: =back
 5230: 
 5231: =head1 COMMON PITFALLS
 5232: 
 5233: The following mistakes cause unexpected behaviour in common usage.
 5234: Read this section before filing a bug report.
 5235: 
 5236: =over 4
 5237: 
 5238: =item B<Default CSV separator is C<!>, not C<,>>
 5239: 
 5240: For historical reasons the module defaults to bang (C<!>) as the
 5241: field separator for CSV files.  Standard RFC 4180 comma-separated
 5242: files require you to pass C<< sep_char => ',' >> in your subclass
 5243: constructor.
 5244: 
 5245:     sub new {
 5246:         my ($class, %args) = @_;
 5247:         return $class->SUPER::new(sep_char => ',', %args);
 5248:     }
 5249: 
 5250: =item B<Primary key column is named C<entry>, not C<key>>
 5251: 
 5252: The default primary-key column name is C<entry> because C<key> is
 5253: a reserved word in SQL.  If your data uses a different column name
 5254: (e.g. C<id>, C<customer_id>), pass C<< id => 'customer_id' >> to
 5255: C<new()>.
 5256: 
 5257: =item B<Duplicate primary keys silently overwrite earlier rows>
 5258: 
 5259: In slurp mode the entire data file is loaded into a Perl hash keyed
 5260: by the C<entry> column.  If two rows share the same key value the
 5261: second row replaces the first.  To keep duplicates either disable
 5262: slurp with C<< max_slurp_size => 0 >> or switch to a C<no_entry>
 5263: schema.
 5264: 
 5265: =item B<Operator-hash criteria do not work on no-DBI backends>
 5266: 
 5267: No-DBI backends (XLSX, DBM::Deep, JSON, HTML URL, Redis) have no SQL
 5268: engine.  Criteria that use operator hashes (e.g.
 5269: C<< score => { '>=' => 80 } >>) trigger the SQL path and will croak
 5270: with a "meaningless on a NoSQL database" error.  Use simple scalar
 5271: criteria (C<< score => 80 >>) on these backends.
 5272: 
 5273: =item B<Read-only results: do not modify returned hashrefs>
 5274: 
 5275: Unless you pass C<< no_fixate => 1 >>, every hashref returned by
 5276: C<selectall_arrayref>, C<fetchrow_hashref>, and similar methods is
 5277: made read-only by C<Data::Reuse>.  Attempting to assign a value to
 5278: a key in the returned hashref will throw an exception.  If you need
 5279: to modify a row, either copy it first (C<my %row = %{$orig_row}>) or
 5280: construct the object with C<< no_fixate => 1 >>.
 5281: 
 5282: =item B<C<where()> calls in the query builder use AND semantics>
 5283: 
 5284: Each call to C<< $q->where(...) >> adds an additional required
 5285: condition.  Two separate C<where()> calls do NOT produce OR
 5286: semantics.  To express OR, pass C<< -or => [...] >> inside a single
 5287: C<where()> call:
 5288: 
 5289:     # Wrong: this is AND, not OR
 5290:     $q->where(status => 'active')->where(status => 'pending');
 5291: 
 5292:     # Correct: OR grouping inside one where() call
 5293:     $q->where(-or => [{ status => 'active' }, { status => 'pending' }]);
 5294: 
 5295: =item B<C<aggregate()> C<sort_by> must name a grouped or aliased column>
 5296: 
 5297: The C<sort_by> parameter for C<aggregate()> only accepts column names
 5298: from the C<by> list or measure C<as> aliases.  You cannot sort
 5299: aggregate results by a raw data column that is not part of the group.
 5300: This restriction applies equally to the C<< ->order_by() >> call on a
 5301: query builder that has C<measure()> methods set.
 5302: 
 5303: =item B<C<sort_by> is not an argument to the query builder's C<all()>>
 5304: 
 5305: When using the fluent query builder to build an aggregate query, set
 5306: the sort column via C<< ->order_by('col') >> before calling
 5307: C<< ->all() >>.  Passing C<< ->all(sort_by => 'col') >> has no
 5308: effect because the C<all()> terminal method ignores extra arguments.
 5309: 
 5310: =item B<C<Params::Get> positional mapping with C<no_entry = 0>>
 5311: 
 5312: When C<no_entry> is false (the default), the first positional argument
 5313: to C<selectall_arrayref> and C<fetchrow_hashref> is silently mapped to
 5314: the C<entry> key.  Passing an operator-hash criteria in that position
 5315: (e.g. C<selectall_arrayref(status => { '!=' => 'x' })>) causes
 5316: C<status> to be treated as the entry value rather than a column name,
 5317: which will produce wrong results.  Always pass a hashref of criteria
 5318: as a single reference, or use C<no_entry => 1> in your subclass.
 5319: 
 5320: =back
 5321: 
 5322: =head1 ENCODING
 5323: 
 5324: =over 4
 5325: 
 5326: =item B<Column names> must match the pattern C<[a-zA-Z_][a-zA-Z0-9_.]*>.
 5327: Only ASCII letters, digits, underscores, and dots (for qualified names
 5328: such as C<table.column>) are accepted.  Non-ASCII Unicode characters
 5329: in column names will cause a croak with an "unsafe column name" error.
 5330: 
 5331: =item B<Column values> (data stored in your rows) are passed through
 5332: unchanged.  Full UTF-8 strings, emoji, and any valid Perl string are
 5333: safe as values in criteria and are stored and returned as-is.  The
 5334: exact encoding depends on your backend:
 5335: 
 5336: =over 4
 5337: 
 5338: =item * B<SQLite / DBI backends> -- DBD::SQLite stores strings as
 5339: UTF-8 internally.  Enable C<< sqlite_unicode => 1 >> in your DSN
 5340: or connect attributes if you want Perl to receive decoded Unicode
 5341: scalars rather than raw UTF-8 bytes.
 5342: 
 5343: =item * B<CSV / PSV / TSV> -- Text::CSV and DBD::CSV pass bytes
 5344: through unchanged.  If your file is UTF-8-encoded, strings are
 5345: returned as UTF-8 byte strings.  You may need to decode them with
 5346: C<Encode::decode('UTF-8', $val)> in your application.
 5347: 
 5348: =item * B<JSON> -- JSON::MaybeXS decodes JSON strings to Perl Unicode
 5349: scalars automatically.  UTF-8 and emoji in JSON values are fully
 5350: supported.
 5351: 
 5352: =item * B<XML> -- XML::Simple decodes the file with the XML encoding
 5353: declaration.  UTF-8 encoded XML (the default) produces Perl Unicode
 5354: scalars.
 5355: 
 5356: =item * B<XLSX> -- Spreadsheet::ParseXLSX decodes strings to Perl
 5357: Unicode scalars.  Emoji in cell values are fully supported.
 5358: 
 5359: =item * B<HTML URL> -- LWP::UserAgent fetches bytes; HTML::TableExtract
 5360: returns them as-is.  Decode with Encode if the page is UTF-8.
 5361: 
 5362: =back
 5363: 
 5364: =item B<File paths> are handled by C<File::Spec::catfile> and work
 5365: on any platform.  On Linux and macOS, UTF-8 directory and file names
 5366: are fully supported.
 5367: 
 5368: =back
 5369: 
 5370: =head1 KNOWN LIMITATIONS
 5371: 
 5372: =over 4
 5373: 
 5374: =item *
 5375: 
 5376: B<Read-only.>  No INSERT, UPDATE, or DELETE is provided.  C<execute()>
 5377: runs raw read-only SQL.
 5378: 
 5379: =item *
 5380: 
 5381: B<Default CSV separator is C<!>>, not C<,>, for historical reasons.
 5382: Pass C<< sep_char => ',' >> for standard RFC 4180 files.
 5383: 
 5384: =item *
 5385: 
 5386: B<Primary-key column is named C<entry>>, not C<key>, because C<key>
 5387: is a SQL reserved word.  Override with the C<id> parameter.
 5388: 
 5389: =item *
 5390: 
 5391: B<XML slurp is limited.>  Only simple flat XML structures are supported
 5392: in slurp mode.  Multi-key or deeply nested documents will croak.
 5393: Force SQL mode with C<< max_slurp_size => 0 >> if slurp fails.
 5394: 
 5395: =item *
 5396: 
 5397: B<Unique key assumption in slurp mode.>  Duplicate values in the key
 5398: column silently overwrite earlier rows.  Disable slurp with
 5399: C<< max_slurp_size => 0 >> if duplicates are expected.
 5400: 
 5401: =item *
 5402: 
 5403: B<BerkeleyDB does not support joins or the chained query builder.>
 5404: 
 5405: =item *
 5406: 
 5407: B<Column names must be valid SQL identifiers> (letters, digits,
 5408: underscores, and a single dot for C<table.column> join notation).
 5409: Other characters will cause a croak.
 5410: 
 5411: =item *
 5412: 
 5413: B<count() cache is opportunistic.>  Count results are served from cache
 5414: only when a prior C<selectall_arrayref()> or C<count()> call with the
 5415: same criteria has already populated it.
 5416: 
 5417: =back
 5418: 
 5419: =head1 SEE ALSO
 5420: 
 5421: =over 4
 5422: 
 5423: =item * L<Database::Abstraction::Query> - chained query builder
 5424: 
 5425: =item * L<Configure an Object at Runtime|Object::Configure>
 5426: 
 5427: =item * L<JSON::MaybeXS> - JSON backend (optional; install for C<.json> support)
 5428: 
 5429: =item * L<Test Dashboard|https://nigelhorne.github.io/Database-Abstraction/coverage/>
 5430: 
 5431: =back
 5432: 
 5433: =head1 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 5434: 
 5435: The following section uses Z-notation to describe the key state
 5436: transitions and invariants for the module.  Z is a formal
 5437: specification language based on set theory and predicate logic.
 5438: Unicode is used here as permitted by the documentation skill.
 5439: 
 5440: =head2 Database object state
 5441: 
 5442:     State == [
 5443:         data       : TABLE_NAME --> (ENTRY --> ROW) | seq ROW | undef,
 5444:         dbh        : TABLE_NAME --> DBI_HANDLE | undef,
 5445:         type       : Str | undef,
 5446:         base_crit  : CRITERIA,
 5447:         _columns   : seq Str | undef,
 5448:         _schema    : NAME --> COL_INFO | undef,
 5449:         _destroyed : Bool
 5450:     ]
 5451: 
 5452:     Initial state S0 == [
 5453:         data?        = undef,
 5454:         dbh?         = undef,
 5455:         type?        = undef,
 5456:         base_crit?   = {},
 5457:         _columns?    = undef,
 5458:         _schema?     = undef,
 5459:         _destroyed?  = false
 5460:     ]
 5461: 
 5462: =head2 _open_table
 5463: 
 5464:     _open_table ___________________________________________
 5465:     DeltaState
 5466:     class? : CLASS
 5467:     dir?   : DIRPATH | undef
 5468:     â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5469:     pre:  _destroyed = false
 5470:     pre:  dbh(table) = undef AND data(table) = undef
 5471:     post: (EXISTS f : FILE_FORMATS WITH f.probe(dir) = true
 5472:            IMPLIES (f.slurp? IMPLIES data'(table) != undef)
 5473:                 AND (f.dbi?  IMPLIES dbh'(table)  != undef))
 5474: 
 5475: =head2 selectall_arrayref
 5476: 
 5477:     selectall_arrayref __________________________________
 5478:     XiState  (read-only; state unchanged except cache)
 5479:     criteria? : CRITERIA
 5480:     â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5481:     pre:  _destroyed = false
 5482:     result == (data != undef AND simple(criteria)
 5483:                IMPLIES { r : data | match(r, criteria) }
 5484:                ELSE    dbh != undef
 5485:                IMPLIES SQL_SELECT(dbh, criteria))
 5486:     post: result : seq ROW UNION {undef}
 5487: 
 5488: =head2 aggregate
 5489: 
 5490:     aggregate ____________________________________________
 5491:     XiState
 5492:     by?       : seq COLNAME
 5493:     measures? : seq MEASURE_SPEC
 5494:     criteria? : CRITERIA
 5495:     â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5496:     pre:  measures != empty
 5497:     pre:  FORALL m : measures WITH m.as NOT IN by
 5498:     pre:  sort_by != undef IMPLIES sort_by IN ran(by) UNION aliases(measures)
 5499:     result == (dbh != undef
 5500:                IMPLIES SQL_GROUP_BY(dbh, by, measures, criteria)
 5501:                ELSE    PERL_FOLD(selectall_arrayref(criteria), by, measures))
 5502:     post: result : seq ROW (keys = by UNION aliases(measures))
 5503:     post: FORALL r : result WITH
 5504:               r.count != undef IMPLIES r.count >= 0
 5505:             AND r.sum = undef IMPLIES no rows in that group
 5506: 
 5507: =head2 DESTROY
 5508: 
 5509:     DESTROY ______________________________________________
 5510:     DeltaState
 5511:     â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5512:     post: _destroyed' = true
 5513:     post: dbh'      = undef  (DBI handles disconnected)
 5514:     post: data'     = undef
 5515:     post: _columns' = undef
 5516:     post: _schema'  = undef
 5517: 
 5518: =head1 STATE DIAGRAM
 5519: 
 5520: The following ASCII diagram shows every valid state the object can
 5521: occupy and the transitions between them.  Transitions are labelled
 5522: with the method call or event that triggers them.  Actions are shown
 5523: in parentheses.
 5524: 
 5525:     .-----------------------------------------------------------.
 5526:     |                  S1: CONSTRUCTED                          |
 5527:     |  dbh=undef, data=undef, type=undef                        |
 5528:     '---.-------.-------.-------.-------.-----.---.-------------'
 5529:         |       |       |       |       |     |   |
 5530:         | first query call triggers _open_table()
 5531:         |       |       |       |       |     |   |
 5532:         v       v       v       v       v     v   v
 5533:     .------. .------. .------. .------. .--. .--. .--.
 5534:     | S2   | | S3   | | S4   | | S5   | |S6| |S7| |S8|
 5535:     | DBI  | | CSV  | | XML  | | JSON | |Xls| |Bk| |Dp|
 5536:     '---^--' '--^---' '--^---' '--^---' '-^-' '-^-' '-^-'
 5537:         |       |       |       |       |   |   |   |
 5538:         |       |       '-------'-------'   |   |   |
 5539:         |       |         slurp path        |   |   |
 5540:         |       '-----------+---------------'   |   |
 5541:         |                   |                   |   |
 5542:         |         (data populated,              |   |
 5543:         |          _columns/_schema undef)      |   |
 5544:         |                                       |   |
 5545:         | (dbh populated,                       |   |
 5546:         |  data undef)                          |   |
 5547:         |                                       |   |
 5548:         +-------+--------+-----------+----------+   |
 5549:                 |        |           |              |
 5550:           selectall   fetchrow    aggregate      fetchrow
 5551:           count       _hashref    query->all     _hashref
 5552:           each_row    AUTOLOAD    AUTOLOAD        AUTOLOAD
 5553:                 |        |           |              |
 5554:                 v        v           v              v
 5555:     .-----------------------------------------------------------.
 5556:     |                  (result returned to caller)              |
 5557:     |          state is unchanged; only cache may be updated    |
 5558:     '-----------------------------------------------------------'
 5559:                                 |
 5560:                     DESTROY (or object goes out of scope)
 5561:                                 |
 5562:                                 v
 5563:     .-----------------------------------------------------------.
 5564:     |                  S_DEAD: DESTROYED                        |
 5565:     |  _destroyed=true; all handles disconnected;               |
 5566:     |  temp files unlinked; _remote_tmpdir removed              |
 5567:     '-----------------------------------------------------------'
 5568: 
 5569:     State labels:
 5570:       S1  CONSTRUCTED  - new() returned; no I/O done yet (lazy open)
 5571:       S2  DBI          - live DBI handle (SQLite file, DSN, DBD::CSV)
 5572:       S3  CSV_SLURP    - small CSV/PSV/TSV/gzip-CSV slurped into RAM hash
 5573:       S4  XML_SLURP    - XML file slurped into RAM hash
 5574:       S5  JSON_SLURP   - JSON file slurped into RAM hash
 5575:       S6  XLSX_SLURP   - XLSX file slurped (type='XLSX', no DBI handle)
 5576:       S7  BERKELEYDB   - BerkeleyDB key-value file open (berkeley flag set)
 5577:       S8  DEEP_SLURP   - DBM::Deep file slurped into plain RAM hash
 5578: 
 5579:     Blocked transitions (croak before state changes):
 5580:       S1 -> any  if constructor arguments are invalid
 5581:                  (unsafe id/table/host/url)
 5582:       S* -> S*   aggregate() with join on S3/S4/S5/S6/S7/S8
 5583:                  (no DBI handle)
 5584:       S7 -> S*   join() or select()-projection on BerkeleyDB
 5585:       S8 -> S*   join() on DBM::Deep
 5586:
